We begin this appendix by laying out the notation that will be used throughout. This is done in Section
A.1
, which in Section
A.2
is followed by a discussion of some conditions that ensure that the asymptotic distribution of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
is correctly centered at zero. Section
A.3
provides an extra empirical study using the US bank data. Section
A.4
describes the outline of the proofs. Sections
A.5
and
A.6
provide some auxiliary lemmas and their proofs, respectively. Proofs of the main results are provided in Section
A.7
.

A.1
Notation

The matrices
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F^{0}}
and
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\Gamma^{0}}
have been defined in Assumption
1
. In this appendix, we use
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F_{g}^{0}}
and
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\Gamma_{g}^{0}}
to denote the sub-matrices of
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F^{0}}
and
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\Gamma^{0}}
corresponding to
T^{-\nu_{g}}\mathbf{F}_{g}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{g}^{0}
and
N^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{g}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{g}^{0}
, respectively, for
g=1,\ldots,G
. We also define
\mathbf{V}_{g}=\mathrm{diag}(\widehat{\lambda}_{g,1},\ldots,\widehat{\lambda}_{g,d_{max}})
, where
\widehat{\lambda}_{g,d}
has been defined in Step
2
of the IPC estimation procedure. We partition
\mathbf{F}^{0}=(\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{F}_{g}^{0},\mathbf{F}_{+g}^{0})
and
\mathbf{C}_{T}=\mathrm{diag}(T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{I}_{d_{1}},\ldots,T^{-\nu_{g}/2}\mathbf{I}_{d_{g}},\mathbf{C}_{+g,T})
, where
\mathbf{F}_{+g}^{0}=(\mathbf{F}_{g+1}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{F}_{G}^{0})
is
T\times(d_{g+1}+\cdots+d_{G})
, and
\mathbf{C}_{+g,T}=\mathrm{diag}(T^{-\nu_{g+1}/2}\mathbf{I}_{d_{g+1}},\ldots,T^{-\nu_{G}/2}\mathbf{I}_{d_{G}})
is
(d_{g+1}+\cdots+d_{G})\times(d_{g+1}+\cdots+d_{G})
. We partition
\widehat{\mathbf{F}}
,
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}^{0}
and
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{0}
conformably as
\widehat{\mathbf{F}}=(\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{g},\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{+g})
,
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}^{0}=(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0\prime},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{g,i}^{0\prime},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+g,i}^{0\prime})^{\prime}
and
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{0}=(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{g}^{0},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{+g}^{0})
, respectively.

We introduce
\lambda_{g,d}=T^{-\nu_{g}}\mathbf{h}_{g,d}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{g}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{g}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{g}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{g,d}^{0}
, where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{g}^{0}=N^{-1}\mathbf{F}_{g}^{0}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{g}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{g}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{g}^{0\prime}
and
\mathbf{h}_{g,d}^{0}
is the
d
-th column of
\mathbf{H}_{g}^{0}=N^{-1}T^{(\nu_{g}-\delta)/2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{g}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{g}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{g}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{g}^{0}(\mathbf{V}_{g}^{0})^{-1}
with
\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{g}^{0}
being the
T\times d_{g}
matrix consisting of the first
d_{g}
columns of
\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{g}
and
\mathbf{V}_{g}^{0}
being the leading
d_{g}\times d_{g}
principal submatrix of
\mathbf{V}_{g}
. In other words,
\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{g}^{0}
and
\mathbf{V}_{g}^{0}
are
\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{g}
and
\mathbf{V}_{g}
based on treating the number of factors for each group
g
,
d_{g}
, as known. We also define
\mathbf{H}_{g}=T^{-(\nu_{g}-\delta)/2}\mathbf{H}_{g}^{0}
. In order to appreciate the implication of the difference in normalization with respect to
T
, let us consider
\mathbf{H}_{g}^{0}
. By Assumption
1
,
N^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{g}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{g}^{0}
is asymptotically of full rank, and hence
\|N^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{g}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{g}^{0}\|=O_{P}(1)
. Hence, since

\displaystyle\|T^{-(\nu_{g}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{g}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{g}\|^{2}\leq T^{-\delta}\|\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{g}\|_{2}^{2}T^{-\nu_{g}}\|\mathbf{F}_{g}^{0}\|^{2}=T^{-\nu_{g}}\|\mathbf{F}_{g}^{0}\|^{2}=O_{P}\left(1\right).

(A.1)

and
\|(T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{0})^{-1}\|=O_{P}(1)
as explained under (
A.28
), we can show that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{H}_{g}^{0}\|\leq\|N^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{g}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{g}^{0}\|\|T^{-(\nu_{g}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{g}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{g}^{0}\|\|(T^{-\nu_{g}}\mathbf{V}_{g}^{0})^{-1}\|=O_{P}(1),

(A.2)

which in turn implies

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{H}_{g}\|=T^{-(\nu_{g}-\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{H}_{g}^{0}\|=O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{g}-\delta)/2}).

(A.3)

We further use
\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{g,d}^{0}
to refer to the
d
-th column of
\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{g}^{0}
. In this notation,
\widehat{\lambda}_{g,d}=T^{-\delta}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{g,d}^{0\prime}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{g}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{g,d}^{0}
for
d=1,\ldots,d_{g}
.

We also partition
\mathbf{X}_{i}
as
\mathbf{X}_{i}=(\mathbf{X}_{1,i},\ldots,\mathbf{X}_{d_{x},i})
with
\mathbf{X}_{j,i}
being the
j
-th column of
\mathbf{X}_{i}
. The
j
-th column of
\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{D}_{T}
is therefore given by
T^{-\kappa_{j}/2}\mathbf{X}_{j,i}
. Moreover,
\mathrm{vec}\,\mathbf{A}
,
\mathrm{rank}\,\mathbf{A}
,
\mathrm{span}\,\mathbf{A}
and
\lambda(\mathbf{A})
denote the vectorized version, rank, span and eigenvalues of
\mathbf{A}
, respectively,
a\wedge b=\min\{a,b\}
and
a\vee b=\max\{a,b\}
.

A.2
Conditions that ensure asymptotic unbiasedness

In this section, we provide a set of assumptions that ensure that the asymptotic distribution of
\sqrt{NT}\mathbf{D}_{T}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0})
given in Theorem
3.1
is free of bias without for that matter requiring that
\varepsilon_{i,t}
is serially and cross-sectionally independent. One way to accomplish this is to assume that
\rho_{1}=\rho_{2}=0
, as in Corollary
3.1
. The assumptions considered here, which are stated in Assumption
A.1
, can be seen as alternatives to this last condition. In terms of the notation of Theorem
3.1
, they ensure that
\mathbf{A}_{1}=\mathbf{A}_{2}=\mathbf{0}_{d_{x}\times 1}
.

Assumption A.1

(No asymptotic bias)
.

One of the following set of conditions is met:

(a)

T^{1-\nu_{g}}E(\mathbf{f}_{g,t}^{0\prime}\mathbf{f}_{g,s}^{0}|\mathbf{x}_{t},\mathbf{x}_{s})=\phi_{ts}
w.p.a.1 and
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}|\phi_{ts}|=O(T)
, where
\mathbf{x}_{t}=(\mathbf{x}_{1,t},\ldots,\mathbf{x}_{N,t})^{\prime}
. If
G\geq 2
, then
q<(\nu_{G}+\nu_{G-1})/2-1/4
and
\nu_{g-1}-\nu_{g}>1/2
for
g=2,\ldots,G
.

(b)

T/N\to c_{4}\in(0,\infty)
and
\nu_{G}>1
. If
G\geq 2
, then
q<(\nu_{G}+\nu_{G-1}-1)/2
and
\nu_{g-1}-\nu_{g}>1/2
.

(c)

T^{1-\nu_{g}-\kappa_{j}}E(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}x_{j,i,t}x_{j,k,s}\mathbf{f}_{g,t}^{0\prime}\mathbf{f}_{g,s}^{0})=O(T^{2-r})
, where
r<2
,
r+\nu_{G}-1>0
and
x_{j,i,t}
is the
j
-th row of
\mathbf{x}_{i,t}
. If
G\geq 2
, then
\nu_{g-1}-\nu_{g}>1/2
.

Assumption
A.1
ensures that the asymptotic distribution of
\sqrt{NT}\mathbf{D}_{T}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0})
is bias-free. It is, however, not necessary, and (a)–(b) should therefore be viewed as examples of conditions under which there is no asymptotic distribution bias. These conditions all have their strengths and weaknesses, and so their suitability will in general depend on the context. Take as an example condition (b), which has the advantage of not requiring any more moment conditions than those that are already in Assumption
1
. It does, however, require that
\nu_{G}>1
, which rules out both signal-weak and stationary factors (
\nu_{G}\leq 1
). Conditions (a) and (c) are more general in this regard, but then at the expense of requiring additional moment conditions. Condition (c) is a functional central limit theorem style moment condition.

The next corollary to Theorem
3.1
verifies that the asymptotic distribution of
\sqrt{NT}\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0})
is indeed bias-free under Assumption
A.1
.

Corollary A.1

(Unbiased asymptotic distribution)
.

Suppose that Assumptions
1
–
5
,
7
, and
A.1
are met and that
N/T^{\nu_{G}}\to 0
. Then, as
N,\,T\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{NT}\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0})\to_{D}MN(\mathbf{0}_{d_{x}\times 1},\mathbf{B}_{0}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Omega}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{-1}).

(A.1)

The asymptotic distribution in Corollary
A.1
is the same as the one given in Corollary
3.1
.

If
\rho_{1}
,
\rho_{2}
,
\mathbf{A}_{1}
and
\mathbf{A}_{2}
are all different from zero, one possibility is to use bias correction.
Dhaene and Jochmans 2015
were first to bring attention to the relevance of the half-panel Jackknife approach for bias correction in panel data. These authors focus on the fixed effects case, but
Chen et al. 2021
have shown that the Jackknife can be used to correct for bias also in models with interactive effects. In our setting, the value of
\nu_{G}
is unknown, and therefore the standard Jackknife is not directly applicable. We thus turn to the hybrid half-panel Jackknife correction proposed by
Fernández-Val and Weidner 2018
. The bias-corrected version of the IPC estimator is given by

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\text{bc}}=3\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\frac{1}{2}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1,N}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2,N}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1,T}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2,T}),

(A.2)

where
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
is the standard IPC estimator defined in (
2.10
),
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1,N}
and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2,N}
are defined in the same way but applied to cross-sectional units
\{1,\ldots,\lfloor N/2\rfloor\}
and
\{\lfloor N/2\rfloor+1,\ldots,N\}
, respectively, and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1,T}
and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2,T}
are the IPC estimators applied to odd and even numbered time periods, respectively. The splitting in even and odd numbered time periods is needed in order to account for the behaviour of the factors. See
Fernández-Val and Weidner 2018
for a more detailed discussion of the hybrid Jackknife approach.

A.3
Extra empirical study — Returns to scale

In this section, we estimate the returns to scale (RTS) of US banks, an area that has attracted considerable attention in the empirical production literature (see, for example,
Feng and Serletis 2008
). Let us therefore denote by
CT_{i,t}
the observed total cost of bank
i
during period
t
. The total cost is a function
C(\cdot)
of a
J\times 1
vector of input prices
\mathbf{p}_{i,t}=(p_{1,i,t},\ldots,p_{J,i,t})^{\prime}
and a
L\times 1
vector of output quantities
\mathbf{q}_{i,t}=(q_{1,i,t},\ldots,q_{L,i,t})^{\prime}
. Hence,
CT_{i,t}=C(\mathbf{p}_{i,t},\mathbf{q}_{i,t})
, or when expressed in logs,
\ln CT_{i,t}=\ln C(\mathbf{p}_{i,t},\mathbf{q}_{i,t})
. The RTS is simply the reciprocal of the sum of the output elasticities, which can be estimated for a given
C(\cdot)
. In order to ensure that
C(\cdot)
is linearly homogenous in prices, however, it is very common to first normalize costs and prices by one of the prices,
p_{J,i,t}
say. Hence,
\ln(CT_{i,t}/p_{J,i,t})=\ln C(\mathbf{p}_{i,t}/p_{J,i,t},\mathbf{q}_{i,t})
, which in turn implies that the RTS is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{RTS}=\left(\sum_{j=1}^{L}\frac{\partial\ln C(\mathbf{p}_{i,t}/p_{J,i,t},\mathbf{q}_{i,t})}{\partial\ln q_{j,i,t}}\right)^{-1}.

(A.1)

In order to estimate this quantity, estimates of the partial derivatives are needed. The standard way to obtain such estimates in the literature is to assume that
\ln C(\mathbf{p}_{i,t}/p_{J,i,t},\mathbf{q}_{i,t})
is linear, to estimate the resulting linear relationship between
\ln(CT_{i,t}/p_{J,i,t})
,
\ln(\mathbf{p}_{i,t}/p_{J,i,t})
and
\ln\mathbf{q}_{i,t}
by OLS, and to estimate the partial derivatives by simply replacing the parameters by their OLS estimates. A number of modelling issues then arise. First, as pointed out by
Feng and Zhang 2012
, “[t]here are many … types of … unobserved heterogeneity in the US banking industry, which include, but are not limited to, location-related corporate income tax rate, property tax rate, and personal income tax rate.” Hence, there is a need to control for unobserved heterogeneity. Second, the literature typically assumes that the regressors in
\ln(\mathbf{p}_{i,t}/p_{J,i,t})
and
\ln\mathbf{q}_{i,t}
are stationary, which, as pointed out by
Dong et al. 2021
, is highly unlikely to be the case in practice. There is therefore a need to consider approaches that do not require the regressors to be stationary. Third, to account for the fact that costs are typically trending, many researchers fit their models with linear and quadratic trend terms. Of course, deterministic trends can account for some trending behaviour, but not all. Moreover, the results tend to be highly sensitive to how the trending is modelled, suggesting that this is an important issue (see
Feng and Serletis 2008
, for a discussion).

The discussion of the last paragraph suggests that there is a need for an approach that is general enough to accommodate not only unobserved heterogeneity but also trending behaviour. The proposed IPC approach fits this bill and we will therefore use it in this empirical illustration to the RTS of US commercial banks. The data that we will use for this purpose, which are the same as in
Feng et al. 2017
, are quarterly and cover the period 1986–2005, which means that
T=80
. To avoid the impact of entry and exit, we only include continuously operating large banks with assets of at least one billion USD. This gives us a total of
N=466
banks. The data set contains observations on three input prices and output quantities. They are the wage rate of labour (
p_{1,i,t}
), the price of deposits and purchased funds (
p_{2,i,t}
), the price of physical capital (
p_{3,i,t}
), consumer loans (
q_{1,i,t}
), non-consumer loans, which is composed of industrial, commercial and real estate loans (
q_{2,i,t}
), and securities, including non-loan financial assets (
q_{3,i,t}
). All outputs are deflated by the GDP deflator. Following the previous literature (see, for example,
Stiroh 2000
), total cost (
CT_{i,t}
) is computed as the sum of total salaries and benefits divided by the number of full-time employees, the price of deposits and purchased funds equals total interest expense divided by total deposits and purchased funds, and the price of capital equals expenses on premises and equipment divided by premises and fixed assets. Hence, after normalization by
p_{3,i,t}
, there are six variables included in the model;
\ln(CT_{i,t}/p_{3,i,t})
,
\ln(p_{1,i,t}/p_{3,i,t})
,
\ln(p_{2,i,t}/p_{3,i,t})
,
\ln q_{1,i,t}
,
\ln q_{2,i,t}
and
\ln q_{3,i,t}
.

Figure A.1:
The variables in the RTS illustration.

In order to get a feeling for the trending behaviour of the data, in Figure
A.1
we plot all
N
series for each variable. A few observations are noteworthy. First, there is strong co-movement among the series. This is true for all six variables, including the dependent variable,
\ln(CT_{i,t}/p_{3,i,t})
, which we take as evidence in support of our interactive effects specification. Second, the variables are highly persistent and most are trending over time, suggesting that they are non-stationary. This last observation is supported by a formal augmented Dickey–Fuller (ADF) test (with intercept and linear trend) applied to each series. The highest rejection frequency across all
N
series is obtained for
\ln q_{2,i,t}
and is given by 6.7%, which in turn suggests that the evidence against unit root null hypothesis is weak. In fact, since the ADF test does not account for the multiplicity of the testing problem, the true proportion of (trend-)stationary series is likely to be even lower. Third, it is unclear if the trend in
\ln(CT_{i,t}/p_{3,i,t})
is the same as those in the regressors, which means that it is important to allow the factors to be trending, as otherwise any unaccounted for trend in
\ln(CT_{i,t}/p_{3,i,t})
will be pushed into the regression errors.

Figure A.2:
The estimated factors in the RTS illustration.

We now go on to discuss the actual estimation results, which are reported in Table
A.1
. The estimated model is given by (
2.1
) with
y_{i,t}=\ln(CT_{i,t}/p_{3,i,t})
,
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}=(\beta_{1}^{0},\ldots,\beta_{5}^{0})^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{x}_{i,t}=(\ln(p_{1,i,t}/p_{3,i,t}),\ln(p_{2,i,t}/p_{3,i,t}),\ln q_{1,i,t},\ln q_{2,i,t},\ln q_{3,i,t})^{\prime}
. Hence, in this notation,

\displaystyle\mathrm{RTS}=\frac{1}{\beta_{3}^{0}+\beta_{4}^{0}+\beta_{5}^{0}}

(A.2)

is the object of interest, which we estimate using

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{RTS}}=\frac{1}{\widehat{\beta}_{3}+\widehat{\beta}_{4}+\widehat{\beta}_{5}},

(A.3)

where
\widehat{\beta}_{3}
,
\widehat{\beta}_{4}
and
\widehat{\beta}_{5}
are from
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=(\widehat{\beta}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\beta}_{5})^{\prime}
. The values of
\delta
and
d_{max}
are set as in Section
4
. The value of
\delta
is again arbitrary and does not require justification. As for
d_{max}
, 10 factors should be large enough to capture the true number, yet not “excessively” large (see
Ahn and Horenstein 2013
, for a discussion). In order to enable inference not only for
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}
but also for the RTS, we follow
Feng et al. 2017
and report 95% bootstrap confidence intervals. The estimated factor groups are given by
\widehat{d}_{1}=\widehat{d}_{2}=1
, implying that the estimated number of factors equals
\widehat{d}_{f}=\widehat{d}_{1}+\widehat{d}_{2}=2
. In Figure
A.2
, we plot the estimated factors, denoted
\widehat{f}_{1,t}
and
\widehat{f}_{2,t}
. We see that while both factor estimates are trending,
\widehat{f}_{2,t}
is growing much faster. As a measure of this difference in the trends, we look at
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{\gamma}_{1,i}^{2}/\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{\gamma}_{2,i}^{2}
. By using the results provided in the appendix, we can show that this ratio should be
O_{P}(T^{\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}})
, implying
\ln(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{\gamma}_{1,i}^{2}/\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{\gamma}_{2,i}^{2})/\ln T=\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}+o_{P}(1)
. By plugging in the known value of
T
and
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{\gamma}_{1,i}^{2}/\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{\gamma}_{2,i}^{2}=123.4
, we get
\ln(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{\gamma}_{1,i}^{2}/\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{\gamma}_{2,i}^{2})/\ln T=1.1
, which is thus an estimate of
\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}
. Hence, there is indeed a substantial difference in the degree of trending of the two factors. But it is not as large as it would have been if
\widehat{f}_{1,t}
and
\widehat{f}_{2,t}
were made up of a quadratic and a linear trend, say, in which case
\nu_{1}=5
and
\nu_{2}=3
, such that
\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}=2
. Hence, just as expected given Figure
A.2
, while trending, the behaviour of the factors is not just a simple deterministic function of time, which of course casts doubt on much of the existing research.

While our main interest is in the RTS, for completeness in Table
A.1
, we also report the estimated elasticities. The first thing to note is that the RTS is estimated to be significantly larger than one, suggesting that the large US banks considered here have had increasing returns to scale during the period of investigation. This is consistent with the results reported by, for example,
Feng et al. 2017
and
Wheelock and Wilson 2012
. One explanation for this finding is that the banks have been adopting productivity-enhancing internet technologies, leading to increased RTS. The confidence intervals are very narrow not only for the RTS, but also for the elasticities, which are all significant.

Table A.1:
Empirical results for the RTS illustration.

Notes
:
\widehat{\beta}_{1}
,
\widehat{\beta}_{2}
,
\widehat{\beta}_{3}
,
\widehat{\beta}_{4}
and
\widehat{\beta}_{5}
refer to the estimated IPC slopes of
\ln(p_{i,t}/p_{3,i,t})
,
\ln(p_{2,i,t}/p_{3,i,t})
,
\ln q_{1,i,t}
,
\ln q_{2,i,t}
and
\ln q_{3,i,t}
, respectively, when the dependent variable is
\ln(CT_{i,t}/p_{3,i,t})
. The reported Wald
p
-values for
\widehat{\beta}_{1}
–
\widehat{\beta}_{5}
test the null hypothesis that the relevant coefficient is zero. The
p
-value for RTS test the null hypothesis that
\text{RTS}=1
.

A.4
Outline of the proofs

In this section, we describe the outline of the proofs. We assume throughout that
G\geq 2
. The proofs for the cases when
G\in\{0,1\}
are much simpler, and can be obtained by manipulating the proofs for
G\geq 2
.

Lemma
A.1
is a widely used result for studying the eigenvalues of large dimensional matrices (
Lam et al. 2011
), and is presented here for convenience.

Our own theoretical development is carried out stepwise, starting with Step
1
of the IPC estimation procedure. Lemma
A.2
is very important in this regard. It presents two order results that are used repeatedly throughout the proofs. Given Lemma
A.2
, we are able to establish Lemma
3.1
of the main text, which provides a lower bound on the rate of convergence of the initial Step
1
estimator
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}
.

As a first step towards establishing the consistency of the Step
2
estimators of
(d_{2},\ldots,d_{G})
, in Lemma
A.3
we study limiting behaviour of the eigenvalues associated with
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1}
in (
2.7
). The consistency of
\widehat{d}_{1}
reported in Lemma
3.2
of the main text is a direct consequence of Lemma
A.3
. Lemmas
A.4
and
A.5
enable consistent estimation of
(d_{2},\ldots,d_{G})
, and are thus key in proving Lemma
3.3
of the main text. For simplicity, in the proofs of Lemmas
A.4
and
A.5
we treat
d_{1}
as known, which according to Lemma
3.2
is justified in large samples.

The final step in our theoretical analysis is to prove the asymptotic mixed normality of the Step
3
IPC estimator
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
, as stated in Theorem
3.1
. In so doing, we treat
(d_{1},\ldots,d_{G})
as known, which is again justified in large samples. We begin by proving Lemma
A.6
, which is needed in the proof of Theorem
3.1
. The proofs of Corollaries
3.1
and
A.1
are immediate consequences of that of Theorem
3.1
. We also derive the rate of convergence of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}
under the conditions of Theorem
3.1
. This rate is provided as a part of Lemma
A.6
.

A.5
Auxiliary lemmas

Lemma A.1
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{E}
are
n\times n
symmetric matrices and that
\mathbf{Q}=(\mathbf{Q}_{1},\mathbf{Q}_{2})
, where
\mathbf{Q}_{1}
is
n\times r
and
\mathbf{Q}_{2}
is
n\times(n-r)
, is an orthogonal matrix such that
\text{span}\,\mathbf{Q}_{1}
is an invariant subspace for
\mathbf{A}
. Decompose
\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{Q}
and
\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{Q}
as
\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{Q}=\mathrm{diag}(\mathbf{D}_{1},\mathbf{D}_{2})
and

\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{Q}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{E}_{11}&\mathbf{E}_{12}\\
\mathbf{E}_{21}&\mathbf{E}_{22}\end{array}\right).

Let
\mathrm{sep}(\mathbf{D}_{1},\mathbf{D}_{2})=\min_{\lambda_{1}\in\lambda(\mathbf{D}_{1}),\ \lambda_{2}\in\lambda(\mathbf{D}_{2})}|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|
. If
\mathrm{sep}(\mathbf{D}_{1},\mathbf{D}_{2})>0
and
\|\mathbf{E}\|_{2}\leq\mathrm{sep}(\mathbf{D}_{1},\mathbf{D}_{2})/5
, then there exists a
(n-r)\times r
matrix
\mathbf{P}
with
\|\mathbf{P}\|_{2}\leq 4\|\mathbf{E}_{21}\|_{2}/\mathrm{sep}(\mathbf{D}_{1},\mathbf{D}_{2})
, such that the columns of
\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{0}=(\mathbf{Q}_{1}+\mathbf{Q}_{2}\mathbf{P})(\mathbf{I}_{r}+\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P})^{-1/2}
define an orthonormal basis for a subspace that is invariant for
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{E}
.

Lemma A.2
.

Under Assumption
1
, as
N,\,T\to\infty
,

\mathrm{(a)}

\sup_{\mathbf{F}\in\mathbb{D}_{F}}(NT)^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}_{F}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}=O_{P}(N^{-1}\vee T^{-1})
;

\mathrm{(b)}

\sup_{\mathbf{F}\in\mathbb{D}_{F}}\|(NT)^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{D}_{T}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}_{F}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\|=O_{P}(N^{-1/2}\vee T^{-1/2})
.

If Assumption
3
also holds, then

\mathrm{(c)}

(NT)^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\|=O_{P}(N^{-1/2}\vee T^{-1/2})
and
(NT)^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|=O_{P}(N^{-1/2}\vee T^{-1/2})
;

\mathrm{(d)}

\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|=O_{P}(\sqrt{NT})
.

Lemma A.3
.

Let Assumptions
1
–
4
hold. Then, as
N,\,T\to\infty
,

\mathrm{(a)}

T^{-\nu_{1}}|\widehat{\lambda}_{1,d}-\lambda_{1,d}|=O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2})
for
d=1,\ldots,d_{1}
;

\mathrm{(b)}

T^{-\nu_{1}}|\widehat{\lambda}_{1,d}|=O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})})
for
d=d_{1}+1,\ldots,d_{max}
.

Lemma A.4
.

As
N,\,T\to\infty
, the following hold under Assumptions
1
–
5
:

\mathrm{(a)}

T^{-\delta}\|\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\|=O_{P}(T^{-(\delta+\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}+N^{-1/2}T^{-(\delta+\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}-1)/2}+N^{-(1-p)}T^{-(\delta+\nu_{1}-2\nu_{2})/2}+T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2-q})
;

\mathrm{(b)}

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}-\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{1,i}\|^{2}=O_{P}(N\vee T+NT^{2q-\nu_{1}}+N^{-(1-2p)}T^{\nu_{2}})
.

Lemma A.5
.

Let
\tau_{NT}=N^{-1/2}T^{-(\nu_{2}-1)/2}+T^{-\nu_{2}/2}+T^{-(\nu_{2}-\nu_{3})}+T^{q-(\nu_{1}+\nu_{2})/2}+N^{-(1-p)}
. As
N,\,T\to\infty
, the following hold under the conditions of Lemma
A.4
:

\mathrm{(a)}

T^{-\nu_{2}}|\widehat{\lambda}_{2,d}-\lambda_{2,d}|=O_{P}(\tau_{NT})
for
d=1,\ldots,d_{2}
;

\mathrm{(b)}

T^{-\nu_{2}}|\widehat{\lambda}_{2,d}|=O_{P}(\tau_{NT}^{2})
for
d=d_{2}+1,\ldots,d_{max}
.

Lemma A.6
.

Let Assumptions
1
–
5
hold. In addition, let
NT^{-\nu_{G}}<\infty
, as
N,\,T\to\infty
,

\mathrm{(a)}

\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|=O_{P}((NT)^{-1/2}\vee\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0})\|)
;

\mathrm{(b)}

\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0})\|=O_{P}((NT)^{-1/2})
.

A.6
Proofs of auxiliary lemmas

Proof of Lemma
A.1
.

This is Lemma 3 of
Lam et al. 2011
. The proof is therefore omitted.
\blacksquare

Proof of Lemma
A.2
.

Consider (a). We have

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{F}\in\mathbb{D}_{F}}\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}_{F}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathbf{F}\in\mathbb{D}_{F}}(NT)^{-1}\text{tr}(\mathbf{P}_{F}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\leq O(1)\sup_{\mathbf{F}\in\mathbb{D}_{F}}(NT)^{-1}\|\mathbf{P}_{F}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\sup_{\mathbf{F}\in\mathbb{D}_{F}}(NT)^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{P}(N^{-1}\vee T^{-1}),

(A.1)

where the first inequality follows from the fact that
|\text{tr}\,\mathbf{A}|\leq\text{rank}\,\mathbf{A}\,\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}
, the second inequality follows from the fact that
\|\mathbf{P}_{{F}}\|_{2}=1
, and the second equality hold by Assumption
1
(b).

The result in (b) is due to

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{F}\in\mathbb{D}_{F}}\left\|\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{D}_{T}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}_{F}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{x}}\sup_{\mathbf{F}\in\mathbb{D}_{F}}\left|\text{tr}\,(T^{-\kappa_{j}/2}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{P}_{F}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{x}}\sup_{\mathbf{F}\in\mathbb{D}_{F}}\|T^{-\kappa_{j}/2}\mathbf{X}_{j}\|_{2}\|\mathbf{P}_{F}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=O(1)(NT)^{-1}O_{P}(\sqrt{NT})O_{P}(\sqrt{N}\vee\sqrt{T})

\displaystyle=O_{P}(N^{-1/2}\vee T^{-1/2}),

(A.2)

where, with a slight abuse of notation and in this proof only,
\mathbf{X}_{j}=(\mathbf{X}_{j,1},\ldots,\mathbf{X}_{j,N})^{\prime}
, the second inequality follows from
|\text{tr}\,\mathbf{A}|\leq\text{rank}\,\mathbf{A}\,\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}
, while the first equality is due to Assumption
1
.

For (c), we use

\displaystyle(NT)^{-2}E\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(NT)^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}E[\varepsilon_{i,t}^{2}\varepsilon_{i,s}^{2}]+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j\neq i}E[\varepsilon_{i,t}\varepsilon_{i,s}\varepsilon_{j,t}\varepsilon_{j,s}]\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(NT)^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}E(\varepsilon_{i,t}^{4})+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j\neq i}E[(\varepsilon_{i,t}\varepsilon_{j,t}-\sigma_{\varepsilon,ij})^{2}]\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(NT)^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s\neq t}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}E[\varepsilon_{i,t}^{2}\varepsilon_{i,s}^{2}]+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j\neq i}E[(\varepsilon_{i,t}\varepsilon_{j,t}-\sigma_{\varepsilon,ij})(\varepsilon_{i,s}\varepsilon_{j,s}-\sigma_{\varepsilon,ij})]\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j\neq i}\sigma_{\varepsilon,ij}^{2}=O(N^{-1})+O(T^{-1}),

(A.3)

where the third equality follows from using the mixing condition on
\varepsilon_{i,t}\varepsilon_{j,t}
across
t
. The above result implies that
(NT)^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|=O_{P}(N^{-1/2})+O_{P}(T^{-1/2})
as required for the first result in (c). The second follows from

\displaystyle(NT)^{-2}E\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}T^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}E[\varepsilon_{i,t}\varepsilon_{j,t}\varepsilon_{i,s}\varepsilon_{j,s}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(NT)^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}E[\varepsilon_{i,t}^{2}\varepsilon_{i,s}^{2}]+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j\neq i}E[\varepsilon_{i,t}\varepsilon_{i,s}\varepsilon_{j,t}\varepsilon_{j,s})\right)

\displaystyle=O(N^{-1})+O(T^{-1}),

(A.4)

where the last step follows by the same arguments used to establish the first result of (c).

It remains to prove (d), which is a direct consequence of Assumptions
1
and
3
, as seen from

\displaystyle E\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|^{2}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}E[\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{j}^{0}\varepsilon_{i,t}\varepsilon_{j,t}]\leq O(T)\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}|\sigma_{\varepsilon,ij}|=O(NT).

(A.5)

This establishes (d) and hence the proof of the lemma is complete.
\blacksquare

Proof of Lemma
A.3
.

Consider (a). As in Appendix
A.1
, decompose
\mathbf{F}^{0}=(\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0},\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0})
and
\mathbf{C}_{T}=\mathrm{diag}(T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{I}_{d_{1}},\mathbf{C}_{+1,T})
, where
\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}=(\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{F}_{G}^{0})
is
T\times(d_{f}-d_{1})
and
\mathbf{C}_{+1,T}=\mathrm{diag}(T^{-\nu_{2}/2}\mathbf{I}_{d_{2}},\ldots,T^{-\nu_{G}/2}\mathbf{I}_{d_{G}})
is
(d_{f}-d_{1})\times(d_{f}-d_{1})
. We partition
\widehat{\mathbf{F}}
,
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}^{0}
and
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{0}
conformably as
\widehat{\mathbf{F}}=(\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1},\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{+1})
,
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i}^{0}=(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0\prime},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0\prime})^{\prime}
and
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{0}=(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{+1}^{0})
, respectively.

By the definition of the eigenvectors and eigenvalues,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}=\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}
. By using this and the definition of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1}
,

\displaystyle T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})^{\prime}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})^{\prime}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})^{\prime}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{9}\mathbf{J}_{j},

(A.6)

with implicit definitions of
\mathbf{J}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{J}_{9}
. Note that

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{9}=\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}(N^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0})(T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}).

(A.7)

Hence, moving this term over to the left-hand side, the above expression for
T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}
becomes

\displaystyle T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}-\mathbf{J}_{9}=\sum_{j=1}^{8}\mathbf{J}_{j}.

(A.8)

We now evaluate each of the terms on the right-hand side.

Because
T^{-\delta}\|\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\|^{2}=d_{max}
and
(NT)^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{D}_{T}\|^{2}=O_{p}(1)
by Assumption
1
, the order of
\mathbf{J}_{1}
is given by

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{J}_{1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})^{\prime}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\prime}\right\|T^{-\delta/2}\|\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\|

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{D}_{T}\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{1-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|^{2}).

(A.9)

Moreover, since

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT^{\nu_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}\|^{2}T^{-\nu_{1}}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\|^{2}=O_{P}(1)

(A.10)

by Assumption
1
, we can show that

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{J}_{2}\|

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\|

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\left(\frac{1}{NT^{\delta}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{D}_{T}\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{NT^{\nu_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{(1-\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|),

(A.11)

and by exactly the same arguments,

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{J}_{3}\|=O_{P}(T^{(1-\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|).

(A.12)

For
\mathbf{J}_{4}
, we use

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{D}_{T}\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0})^{\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{NT^{\delta}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{D}_{T}\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{NT^{\nu_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq O_{P}(T^{(1-\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|)O_{P}(T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\|\mathbf{C}_{+1,T}^{-1}\|)

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{(1-\delta-\nu_{1}+\nu_{2})/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|),

(A.13)

where the last equality makes use of the fact that
\|\mathbf{C}_{+1,T}^{-1}\|=O(T^{\nu_{2}/2})
, as
\nu_{2}>\cdots>\nu_{G}
by Assumption
1
. We can further show that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{D}_{T}\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathrm{vec}\,[\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{D}_{T}\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{D}_{T})\right\|\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{(2-\nu_{1}-\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|),

(A.14)

where the last equality holds, because by Assumption
3
and
(\mathrm{tr}\,\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B})^{2}\leq(\mathrm{tr}\,\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{A})(\mathrm{tr}\,\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\mathbf{B})
, we have

\displaystyle E\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\otimes\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{D}_{T})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}T^{\nu_{1}+\delta}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}E(\mathbf{x}_{j,t}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}_{T}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{i,t}\varepsilon_{i,s}\varepsilon_{j,s})\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}T^{\nu_{1}+\delta}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}E|\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{D}_{T}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{D}_{T})|\cdot|\sigma_{\varepsilon,ij}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}T^{\nu_{1}+\delta-2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sqrt{T^{-1}E\|\mathbf{D}_{T}\mathbf{X}_{j}\|^{2}}\sqrt{T^{-1}E\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{D}_{T}\|^{2}}|\sigma_{\varepsilon,ij}|

\displaystyle=O(1)\frac{1}{N^{2}T^{\nu_{1}+\delta-2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}|\sigma_{\varepsilon,ij}|=O(N^{-1}T^{2-\nu_{1}-\delta}).

(A.15)

Hence, by adding the results,

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{J}_{4}\|

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})^{\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{(1-\delta-\nu_{1}+\nu_{2})/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|+N^{-1/2}T^{(2-\nu_{1}-\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|)

\displaystyle=o_{P}(T^{-(1-\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|),

(A.16)

where we have used Assumptions
1
and
4
(
T/N^{2}=O(1)
under
\nu_{G}<1
) to show that
T^{(1-\delta-\nu_{1}+\nu_{2})/2}
and
N^{-1/2}T^{(2-\nu_{1}-\delta)/2}
are
o(1)
. The same steps can be used to show that

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{J}_{5}\|\leq o_{P}(T^{-(1-\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|).

(A.17)

For
\mathbf{J}_{6}
, we use

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{J}_{6}\|

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})^{\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\cdot(N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{+1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{+1}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0\prime}\|+N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|

\displaystyle+2N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{+1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|).

(A.18)

By Assumption
1
,

\displaystyle N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{+1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{+1}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0\prime}\|=O_{P}(T^{\nu_{2}-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}).

(A.19)

Another application of Assumption
1
and Lemma
A.2
gives

\displaystyle N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{1-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}(N^{-1/2}\vee T^{-1/2})),

(A.20)

\displaystyle N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{+1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|

\displaystyle\leq N^{-1/2}T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta-\nu_{2}-1)/2}T^{-\nu_{2}/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\|(NT)^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{+1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta-\nu_{2}-1)/2}).

(A.21)

These results can be inserted into the expression for
T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{J}_{6}\|
, giving

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{J}_{6}\|=O_{P}(T^{\nu_{2}-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}+N^{-1/2}T^{1-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}).

(A.22)

Next up is
\mathbf{J}_{7}
. By using Assumptions
1
and
4
, and Lemma
A.2
, and the arguments use in evaluating
\mathbf{J}_{6}
,

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{J}_{7}\|

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})^{\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq O(1)N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{+1}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0\prime}\|+O(1)N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{(\nu_{2}-\delta)/2}+N^{-1/2}T^{(1-\delta)/2})=O_{P}(T^{(\nu_{2}-\delta)/2}),

(A.23)

and we can similarly show that

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{J}_{8}\|=O_{P}(T^{(\nu_{2}-\delta)/2}).

(A.24)

By putting everything together, (
A.8
) becomes

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}-\mathbf{J}_{9}\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{-(\delta-1)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|)

\displaystyle+O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{1-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2})+O_{P}(T^{-(\delta-\nu_{2})/2}).

(A.25)

We now left multiply (
A.8
) by
T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}^{\prime}
to obtain that

\displaystyle T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{V}_{1}-(T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0})(N^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0})(T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1})

\displaystyle=T^{-(\nu_{1}-\delta)/2}O_{P}(T^{-(\delta-1)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|+N^{-1/2}T^{1-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}+T^{-(\delta-\nu_{2})/2})

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-1)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|)+O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2})

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}),

(A.26)

where the third equality follows from Assumption
4
and Lemma
3.1
. This implies that
\mathbf{V}_{1}
is at most of rank
d_{1}
. Similarly, we can left-multiply (
A.8
) by
T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}
to obtain

\displaystyle\|T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}(T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{V}_{1})-T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\mathbf{J}_{9}\|=O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}),

(A.27)

which in turn implies that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F_{1}^{0}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\Gamma_{1}^{0}}(T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}})=(T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}})(T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{V}_{1})+o_{P}(1).

(A.28)

Note that
T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}
is of rank
d_{1}
, which then further indicates that
\mathbf{V}_{1}
has at least
d_{1}
non-zero elements on the main diagonal which converge to the eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{F_{1}^{0}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\Gamma_{1}^{0}}
. We now can conclude that
\mathbf{V}_{1}
is of rank
d_{1}
in limit.

We are now ready to investigate
\widehat{\lambda}_{1,d}
for
d\leq d_{1}
. Because here
d\leq d_{1}
, we then focus on
\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}^{0}
and
\mathbf{V}_{1}^{0}
, which are defined in Appendix
A.1
. Let us write (
A.8
) as follows:

\displaystyle T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{0}-\mathbf{J}_{9}^{0}=(T^{(\nu_{1}-\delta)/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}^{0}-\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{H}_{1}^{0})(T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{0}),

(A.29)

where
\mathbf{H}_{1}^{0}
is defined in Appendix
A.1
. The above expression implies that (
A.8
) can be written as

\displaystyle T^{(\nu_{1}-\delta)/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}^{0}-\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{H}_{1}^{0}=\sum_{j=1}^{8}\mathbf{J}_{j}^{0}(T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{0})^{-1},

(A.30)

where
\mathbf{J}_{1}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{J}_{9}^{0}
are
\mathbf{J}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{J}_{9}
as defined in (
A.6
), except that now
d_{1}
is taken as known. Note that by the above development

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{8}T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\|\mathbf{J}_{j}^{0}\|=T^{-(\nu_{1}-\delta)/2}\sum_{j=1}^{8}T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{J}_{j}^{0}\|=O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}).

(A.31)

Moreover, since
T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{0}
converges to a full rank matrix by the argument under (
A.28
), we have
\|(T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{0})^{-1}\|=O_{P}(1)
, which in turn implies

\displaystyle T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\|T^{(\nu_{1}-\delta)/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}^{0}-\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{H}_{1}^{0}\|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{8}T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\|\mathbf{J}_{j}^{0}\|\|(T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{0})^{-1}\|=O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}).

(A.32)

This is an important result and in what follows we will use it frequently.

Let us now consider
T^{-\nu_{1}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{1,d}-\lambda_{1,d})
. By the definitions of
\lambda_{1,d}
and
\widehat{\lambda}_{1,d}
given in Section
A.1
,

\displaystyle T^{-\nu_{1}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{1,d}-\lambda_{1,d})

\displaystyle=(T^{-\delta/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1,d}^{0}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0}+T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0})^{\prime}T^{-\nu_{1}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0})

\displaystyle\times(T^{-\delta/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1,d}^{0}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0}+T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0})-T^{-2\nu_{1}}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0}

\displaystyle=(T^{-\delta/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1,d}^{0}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0})^{\prime}T^{-\nu_{1}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0})(T^{-\delta/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1,d}^{0}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0})

\displaystyle+2(T^{-\delta/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1,d}^{0}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0})^{\prime}T^{-\nu_{1}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0})T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0}

\displaystyle+(T^{-\delta/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1,d}^{0}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0})^{\prime}T^{-\nu_{1}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0}(T^{-\delta/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1,d}^{0}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0})

\displaystyle+2(T^{-\delta/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1,d}^{0}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0})^{\prime}T^{-3\nu_{1}/3}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0}

\displaystyle+T^{-2\nu_{1}}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0})\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0}

\displaystyle=J_{1}+2J_{2}+J_{3}+2J_{4}+J_{5},

(A.33)

with obvious definitions of
J_{1},\ldots,J_{5}
. From (
A.32
),

\displaystyle T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\|T^{(\nu_{1}-\delta)/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1,d}^{0}-\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0}\|=\|T^{-\delta/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1,d}^{0}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0}\|=O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}),

(A.34)

which is
o_{P}(1)
under Assumption
1
. This implies
|J_{1}|=o_{P}(|J_{5}|)
,
|J_{2}|=o_{P}(|J_{5}|)
and
|J_{3}|=o_{P}(|J_{4}|)
. It remains to consider
J_{4}
and
J_{5}
. The order of the first of these terms is given by

\displaystyle|J_{4}|

\displaystyle\leq\|T^{-\delta/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1,d}^{0}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0}\|T^{-3\nu_{1}/2}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|T^{-\delta/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1,d}^{0}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0}\|T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\|\|(N^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0})\|T^{-\nu_{1}}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\|\|\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0}\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}),

(A.35)

where we have made use of the fact that
\|\mathbf{H}_{g}^{0}\|=O_{P}(1)
(see Appendix
A.1
), which implies that
\|\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0}\|
is of the same order.

The order of
J_{5}
is the same as that of
J_{4}
. In order to appreciate this, we begin by noting

\displaystyle T^{-\nu_{1}}(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{NT^{\nu_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})^{\prime}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{\nu_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}+\frac{1}{NT^{\nu_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})^{\prime}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{\nu_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{\nu_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})^{\prime}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{\nu_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{\nu_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})^{\prime}+\frac{1}{NT^{\nu_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}.

(A.36)

By the proof for each term of (
A.6
), it is easy to know that

\displaystyle T^{-\nu_{1}}\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0}\|=O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}),

(A.37)

and so

\displaystyle|J_{5}|\leq\|\mathbf{h}_{1,d}^{0}\|^{2}T^{-\nu_{1}}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\|^{2}T^{-\nu_{1}}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0}\|=O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}).

(A.38)

Hence, by putting everything together,

\displaystyle T^{-\nu_{1}}|\widehat{\lambda}_{1,d}-\lambda_{1,d}|\leq|J_{1}|+2|J_{2}|+|J_{3}|+2|J_{4}|+|J_{5}|=O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}),

(A.39)

which establishes (a).

Consider (b). This proof is based on Lemma
A.1
. We therefore start by introducing some notation in order to make the problem here fit the one in Lemma
A.1
. Let us therefore denote by
\mathbf{F}_{1}^{\perp}
a
T\times(d_{max}-d_{1})
matrix such that
T^{-\nu_{1}}(\mathbf{F}_{1}^{\perp},\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{R})^{\prime}(\mathbf{F}_{1}^{\perp},\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{R})=\mathrm{diag}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{max}-d_{1}},\mathbf{I}_{d_{1}})
, where
\mathbf{R}
is a
d_{1}\times d_{1}
rotation matrix. The matrices
T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{\perp}
,
T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{R}
,
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0}
and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0}
correspond to
\mathbf{Q}_{1}
,
\mathbf{Q}_{2}
,
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{E}
of Lemma
A.1
. Our counterpart of the matrix
\mathbf{Q}^{0}_{1}
appearing in this other lemma is thus given by

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{F}}^{\perp}=T^{-\nu_{1}/2}(\mathbf{F}_{1}^{\perp}+\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{P})(\mathbf{I}_{d_{max}-d_{1}}+\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P})^{-1/2},

(A.40)

where

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{\mathrm{sep}(0,T^{-2\nu_{1}}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0})}T^{-\nu_{1}}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0}\|\leq O_{P}(1)T^{-\nu_{1}}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0}\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}).

(A.41)

Since
\widehat{\mathbf{F}}^{\perp}
is an orthonormal basis for a subspace that is invariant for
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1}
, we have
\widehat{\lambda}_{1,d_{1}+d}=\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{d}^{\perp\prime}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{d}^{\perp}
, where
d=1,\ldots,d_{max}-d_{1}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{d}^{\perp}
is the
d
-th column of
\widehat{\mathbf{F}}^{\perp}
. Consider
\|\widehat{\mathbf{F}}^{\perp}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{\perp}\|_{2}
. By the definition of
\widehat{\mathbf{F}}^{\perp}
,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathbf{F}}^{\perp}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{\perp}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\|[\mathbf{F}_{1}^{\perp}+\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{P}-\mathbf{F}_{1}^{\perp}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{max}-d_{1}}+\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P})^{1/2}](\mathbf{I}_{d_{max}-d_{1}}+\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P})^{-1/2}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{\perp}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{max}-d_{1}}-(\mathbf{I}_{d_{max}-d_{1}}+\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P})^{1/2})(\mathbf{I}_{d_{max}-d_{1}}+\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P})^{-1/2}\|_{2}

\displaystyle+T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{max}-d_{1}}+\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P})^{-1/2}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathbf{I}_{d_{max}-d_{1}}-(\mathbf{I}_{d_{max}-d_{1}}+\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P})^{1/2})(\mathbf{I}_{d_{max}-d_{1}}+\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P})^{-1/2}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{max}-d_{1}}+\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P})^{-1/2}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{I}_{d_{max}-d_{1}}-(\mathbf{I}_{d_{max}-d_{1}}+\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P})^{1/2}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{P}\|_{2}\leq 2\|\mathbf{P}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}),

(A.42)

where the second and third inequalities follow from
(
Magnus and Neudecker 2007
, Exercise 1 on page 231)
. This last result can be used to show that

\displaystyle T^{-\nu_{1}}|\widehat{\lambda}_{1,d_{1}+d}|=|\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{d}^{\perp\prime}(T^{-\nu_{1}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1})\widehat{\mathbf{F}}^{\perp}_{d}|

\displaystyle=|(\widehat{\mathbf{F}}^{\perp}_{d}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1,d}^{\perp}+T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1,d}^{\perp})^{\prime}T^{-\nu_{1}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0})(\widehat{\mathbf{F}}^{\perp}_{d}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1,d}^{\perp}+T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1,d}^{\perp})|

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\mathbf{F}}^{\perp}_{d}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1,d}^{\perp}\|^{2}T^{-\nu_{1}}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0}\|

\displaystyle+2\|\widehat{\mathbf{F}}^{\perp}_{d}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1,d}^{\perp}\|T^{-\nu_{1}}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0}\|T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{1,d}^{\perp}\|+\|\widehat{\mathbf{F}}^{\perp}_{d}-T^{-\nu_{1}/2}\mathbf{F}_{1,d}^{\perp}\|^{2}T^{-\nu_{1}}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{1}^{0}\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})}),

(A.43)

where
\mathbf{F}_{1,d}^{\perp}
is the
d
-th column of
\mathbf{F}_{1}^{\perp}
, and the last equality follows from (
A.6
) and the proof of part (a). This completes the proof of the lemma.
\blacksquare

Proof of Lemma
A.4
.

For (a), we take the same starting point as in the proof of part (a) in Lemma
A.3
, which is (
A.8
) with
\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}
and
\mathbf{V}_{1}
based on treating
d_{1}
as known. The rationale for doing so is, as already explained in Appendix
A.4
, that
\widehat{d}_{1}
is consistent. Pre-multiplying this equation through by
T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}
gives

\displaystyle T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}-T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{J}_{9}

\displaystyle=T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}-T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}(N^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0})(T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{8}T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{J}_{j},

(A.44)

or

\displaystyle T^{-\delta}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}-T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{J}_{9}(T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{V}_{1})^{-1}

\displaystyle=T^{-\delta}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}-T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}(N^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0})(T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1})(T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{V}_{1})^{-1}

\displaystyle=T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}\sum_{j=1}^{8}T^{-(\delta+\nu_{2})/2}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{J}_{j}(T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{V}_{1})^{-1},

(A.45)

Under Assumption
5
, the orders of
\mathbf{J}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{J}_{6}
are the same as in those in the proof of the first result of Lemma
A.3
. The stated orders of
\mathbf{J}_{7}
and
\mathbf{J}_{8}
are, however, not sharp and can be improved upon. The order of
T^{-(\delta+\nu_{2})/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{J}_{7}\|
is given by

\displaystyle T^{-(\delta+\nu_{2})/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{J}_{7}\|

\displaystyle\leq O_{P}(1)T^{-\nu_{2}/2}\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}(\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})^{\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\|\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{+1}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0\prime}\|

\displaystyle+O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\|\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(N^{-(1-p)}T^{q-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2})+O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{q-(\nu_{2}+\nu_{1}+\delta-1)/2}),

(A.46)

where the last equality follows from Assumption
5
and Lemma
A.2
. We can similarly show that

\displaystyle T^{-(\delta+\nu_{2})/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{J}_{8}\|

\displaystyle\leq O_{P}(1)T^{-\nu_{2}/2}\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}(\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2,i}^{0}+\mathbf{F}_{+2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+2,i}^{0}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}}\|\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{2}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\|

\displaystyle+O_{P}(1)N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{2}+\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{+2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{+2}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\|

\displaystyle+O_{P}(1)N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{2}+\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(N^{-(1-p)}T^{-(\delta-\nu_{2})/2})+O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{-(\delta-1)/2}),

(A.47)

where
\mathbf{F}_{+2}^{0}
and
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{+2}^{0}
are defined analogously to
\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}
and
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{+1}^{0}
in the proof of Lemma
A.3
. By using these last two results together with the orders of
\mathbf{J}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{J}_{6}
given in the proof of the first result of Lemma
A.3
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{8}T^{-(\delta+\nu_{2})/2}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{J}_{j}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq O_{P}(T^{-(\delta-1)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|)+O_{P}(T^{\nu_{2}-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2})+O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{1-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2})

\displaystyle+O_{P}(N^{-(1-p)}T^{-(\delta-\nu_{2})/2})+O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{-(\delta-1)/2})

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{-\delta/2}+N^{-1/2}T^{-(\delta-1)/2}+N^{-(1-p)}T^{-(\delta-\nu_{2})/2}),

(A.48)

where we have used
\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|=O_{P}(N^{-1/2}\vee T^{-1/2})
of Lemma
3.1
, and Assumptions
3
and
5
. Hence,

\displaystyle\|T^{-\delta}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}-T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{J}_{9}(T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{V}_{1})^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\leq T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{8}T^{-(\delta+\nu_{2})/2}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{J}_{j}\right\|\|(T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{V}_{1})^{-1}\|

\displaystyle=T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}[O_{P}(T^{-\delta/2}+N^{-1/2}T^{-(\delta-1)/2}+N^{-(1-p)}T^{-(\delta-\nu_{2})/2})]

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{-(\delta+\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}+N^{-1/2}T^{-(\delta+\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}-1)/2}+N^{-(1-p)}T^{-(\delta+\nu_{1}-2\nu_{2})/2}),

(A.49)

which together with Assumption
5
yields

\displaystyle\|T^{-\delta}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{J}_{9}\|\|(T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{V}_{1})^{-1}\|

\displaystyle+T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{8}T^{-(\delta+\nu_{2})/2}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\mathbf{J}_{j}\right\|\|(T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{V}_{1})^{-1}\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{q-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}+T^{-(\delta+\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2}+N^{-1/2}T^{-(\delta+\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}-1)/2}

\displaystyle+N^{-(1-p)}T^{-(\delta+\nu_{1}-2\nu_{2})/2}),

(A.50)

as was to be shown for (a).

Let us now consider (b). Analogously to the proof of (a), by invoking Assumption
5
we can improve the orders of
\mathbf{J}_{7}
and
\mathbf{J}_{8}
. For
\mathbf{J}_{7}
,

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{J}_{7}\|

\displaystyle\leq O_{P}(1)N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{+1}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0\prime}\|+O_{P}(1)N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(N^{-(1-p)}T^{-(\delta-\nu_{2})/2})+O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{-(\delta-1)/2}),

(A.51)

where the equality follows from Assumption
5
and Lemma
A.2
. For
\mathbf{J}_{8}
,

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{J}_{8}\|\leq O_{P}(N^{-(1-p)}T^{-(\delta-\nu_{2})/2})+O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{-(\delta-1)/2}).

(A.52)

This implies that the result in (
A.32
) changes to (after replacing
T^{-\nu_{1}/2}
by
T^{-\delta/2}
)

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|T^{(\nu_{1}-\delta)/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}-\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\mathbf{H}_{1}^{0}\|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{8}T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{J}_{j}\|\|(T^{-\nu_{1}}\mathbf{V}_{1})^{-1}\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{-\delta/2}+N^{-1/2}T^{-(\delta-1)/2}+N^{-(1-p)}T^{-(\delta-\nu_{2})/2}).

(A.53)

Hence, since
\mathbf{H}_{1}^{0}
is invertible with
\|\mathbf{H}_{1}^{0}\|=O_{P}(1)
and
\mathbf{H}_{1}=T^{-(\nu_{1}-\delta)/2}\mathbf{H}_{1}^{0}
,

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\mathbf{H}_{1}^{-1}-\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\|=O_{P}(T^{-\delta/2}+N^{-1/2}T^{-(\delta-1)/2}+N^{-(1-p)}T^{-(\delta-\nu_{2})/2}).

(A.54)

We are now ready to consider
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}-\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{1,i}\|^{2}
.

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}-\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{1,i}\|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}-T^{-\delta}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbf{y}_{i}-\mathbf{X}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\|\mathbf{P}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|^{2}+\|\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}\|^{2}+\|\mathbf{P}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}\|^{2}+\|\mathbf{P}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\|^{2}].

(A.55)

We now evaluate each of the terms on the right-hand side one by one. Making use of Assumption
1
and Lemma
3.1
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{P}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{D}_{T}\|^{2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(NT)O_{P}(N^{-1}\vee T^{-1})=O_{P}(N\vee T),

(A.56)

and by another application of Lemma
A.2
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{P}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\|^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\|^{2}=O_{P}(N\vee T).

(A.57)

For
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}\|^{2}
, we use (
A.54
) from which it follows that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}(\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}-\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\mathbf{H}_{1}^{-1})\|^{2}\leq T^{\delta}(T^{-\delta}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}-\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\mathbf{H}_{1}^{-1}\|^{2})

\displaystyle=O_{P}(1)+O_{P}(N^{-1}T)+O_{P}(N^{-2(1-p)}T^{\nu_{2}}),

(A.58)

which in turn implies

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}\|^{2}=O_{P}(N)+O_{P}(T)+O_{P}(N^{-(1-2p)}T^{\nu_{2}}).

(A.59)

For
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{P}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0}\|^{2}
, we use the result given in part (a), giving

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{P}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2,i}^{0}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|T^{-\delta}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2,i}^{0}\|^{2}=O_{P}(NT^{\delta})\|T^{-\delta}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(NT^{\delta})[O_{P}(T^{q-(\nu_{1}+\delta)/2})+O_{P}(T^{-(\delta+\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2})

\displaystyle+O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{-(\delta+\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}-1)/2})+O_{P}(N^{-(1-p)}T^{-(\delta+\nu_{1}-2\nu_{2})/2})]^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(N)[O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})/2})+O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}-1)/2})

\displaystyle+O_{P}(N^{-(1-p)}T^{-(\nu_{1}-2\nu_{2})/2})+O_{P}(T^{q-\nu_{1}/2})]^{2}.

(A.60)

The order of
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{P}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\mathbf{F}_{+2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+2,i}^{0}\|^{2}
is the same. Hence, by adding the above results, (b) follows after simple algebra. The proof is now complete.
\blacksquare

Proof of Lemma
A.5
.

Let
\mathbf{U}_{i}=\mathbf{F}_{+2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+2,i}^{0}+\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}-\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{1,i}
. In this notation,

\displaystyle T^{-(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}\mathbf{V}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[(\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})+\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2,i}^{0}+\mathbf{U}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})

\displaystyle\times(\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})+\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2,i}^{0}+\mathbf{U}_{i}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i})^{\prime}]\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})^{\prime}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2,i}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2,i}^{0}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})^{\prime}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\mathbf{U}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{U}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})^{\prime}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})^{\prime}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2,i}^{0}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2,i}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{U}_{i}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{U}_{i}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2,i}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2,i}^{0}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{U}_{i}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}+\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2,i}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2,i}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{16}\mathbf{K}_{j},

(A.61)

where
\mathbf{K}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{K}_{16}
are implicitly defined. Analogously to the proof of the first result of Lemma
A.3
we move
\mathbf{K}_{16}=\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0}(N^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{2}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{2}^{0})(T^{-(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2})
over to the left, giving

\displaystyle T^{-(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}\mathbf{V}_{2}-\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0}(N^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{2}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{2}^{0})(T^{-(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2})=\sum_{j=1}^{15}\mathbf{K}_{j}.

(A.62)

By using the same steps employed in the proof of the first result of Lemma
A.3
, we can show that

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{K}_{1}+\mathbf{K}_{2}+\mathbf{K}_{3}\|=O_{P}(T^{-(\delta-1)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|).

(A.63)

For
\mathbf{K}_{4}
,

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{K}_{4}\|=T^{-\delta/2}\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\mathbf{U}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\left(\frac{1}{NT^{\delta}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{1}{NT^{\nu_{2}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{F}_{+2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2,i}^{0}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{NT^{\nu_{2}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}-\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{1,i}\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=O_{P}(1)T^{-(\delta-1)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|[O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{2}-\nu_{3})})+O_{P}(T^{-\nu_{2}})+O_{P}(N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{2}-1)})

\displaystyle+O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})})+O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{2}+\nu_{1}-2q)})+O_{P}(N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}-2)})+O_{P}(N^{-2(1-p)})]

\displaystyle=o_{P}(T^{-(\delta-1)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|),

(A.64)

where the second equality follows Lemma
A.4
, and the third follows from Assumptions
1
,
3
, and
5
. The same arguments can be used to show that

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{K}_{5}\|=o_{P}(T^{-(\delta-1)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|).

(A.65)

For
\mathbf{K}_{6}
,

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{K}_{6}\|

\displaystyle\leq T^{-\delta/2}\|\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}\|\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{-(\delta+\nu_{2}-2)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|)

\displaystyle=o_{P}(T^{-(\delta-1)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|),

(A.66)

where the development is similar to (
A.6
). The order of
T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{K}_{7}\|
is the same.

For
\mathbf{K}_{8}
,

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{K}_{8}\|

\displaystyle=T^{-\delta/2}\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}\right\|\leq T^{-\delta/2}\|\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}\|N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(T^{1-(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}(N^{-1/2}\vee T^{-1/2})),

(A.67)

where the last equality holds by Lemma
A.2
.

Further use of Lemma
A.2
gives

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{K}_{9}\|

\displaystyle=T^{-\delta/2}\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2,i}^{0}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}\right\|\leq N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}\|\mathbf{F}_{2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{2}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|T^{-\delta/2}\|\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{-(\delta-1)/2}),

(A.68)

and we can show that
T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{K}_{10}\|
is of the same order.

\mathbf{K}_{11}
requires more work. We begin by expanding it in the following way:

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{K}_{11}\|=T^{-\delta/2}\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}(\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}-\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{1,i}+\mathbf{F}_{+2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2,i}^{0})^{\prime}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}(\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0}-\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}T^{-\delta}\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbf{y}_{i}-\mathbf{X}_{i}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})+\mathbf{F}_{+2}^{0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{2,i}^{0})^{\prime}\right\|T^{-\delta/2}\|\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{2}\|

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})^{\prime}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\right\|

\displaystyle+O(1)\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1,i}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0\prime}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\right\|+O(1)\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+1,i}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{+1}^{0\prime}\mathbf{P}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\right\|

\displaystyle+O(1)\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\right\|+O(1)\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{+2,i}^{0\prime}\mathbf{F}_{+2}^{0\prime}\right\|

\displaystyle=O(1)(K_{111}+K_{112}+K_{113}+K_{114}+K_{115}),

(A.69)

where, similarly to the analysis of
\mathbf{K}_{6}
and using
\|\mathbf{P}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\|_{2}=1
,

\displaystyle K_{111}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{NT^{(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})^{\prime}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\prime}\right\|=O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{-(\delta+\nu_{2}-2)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|)

\displaystyle=o_{P}(T^{-(\delta-1)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|).

(A.70)

Also, making use of (
A.6
), we can show that

\displaystyle K_{112}

\displaystyle\leq N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1}^{0\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|\|\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{F}_{1}}\mathbf{F}_{1}^{0}\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{-(\nu_{2}+\delta-1)/2})+O_{P}(N^{-1}T^{1-(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2})+O_{P}(N^{-(3/2-p)}T^{-(\delta-1)/2}).

(A.71)

Similarly, for
K_{113}
, we can show that

\displaystyle K_{113}=o_{P}(K_{112})+O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{q-(\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\delta-1)/2}),

(A.72)

where we have used Lemmas
A.2
and
A.4
, and Assumption
1
. Further use of Lemma
A.2
shows that
K_{114}
is of the following order:

\displaystyle K_{114}\leq N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\|=O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{2}+\delta-2)/2}(N^{-1/2}\vee T^{-1/2})),

(A.73)

while the order of
K_{115}
is

\displaystyle K_{115}=N^{-1}T^{-(\nu_{2}+\delta)/2}\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{+2}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{+2}^{0\prime}\|=O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{-(\nu_{2}+\delta-1-\nu_{3})/2}).

(A.74)

By inserting the above results into (
A.6
), we obtain

\displaystyle T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{K}_{11}\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(N^{-(3/2-p)}T^{-(\delta-1)/2})+O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{q-(\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\delta-1)/2})

\displaystyle+O_{P}(T^{-(\nu_{2}+\delta-2)/2}(N^{-1/2}\vee T^{-1/2}))+O_{P}(N^{-1/2}T^{-(\nu_{2}+\delta-1-\nu_{3})/2})

\displaystyle+o_{P}(T^{-(\delta-1)/2}\|\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{0}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\|).

(A.75)

The order of
T^{-\delta/2}\|\mathbf{K}_{12}\|
is the same, which can be shown using the above steps.

We move on to
\mathbf{K}_{13}
